15. THE ACCOUNTANT GENERAL IN KARNATAKA
(ENTITLEMENT AND ACCOUNTS)
PARK HOUSE, BANGALORE-01. ... RESPONDENTS
(BY SRI.E.S.INDIRESH, AGA FOR R1 TO R15)
THESE WRIT PETITIONS ARE FILED UNDER ARTICLE 226
OF THE CONSTITUTION OF INDIA PRAYING TO ISSUE A WRIT
OF MANDAMUS OR ANY OTHER APPROPRIATE WRIT BY
STRIKING DOWN THE KARNATAKA EDUCATION AMENDMENT
ACT NO.4 OF 2015 DATED 9.1.2015 PASSED BY RESPONDENT
STATE, AS THE SAME IS UNCONSTITUTIONAL SO FAR AS IT
RELATES TO PETITIONERS HEREIN AT ANNEXURE –A. DIRECT
RESPONDENTS TO EXTEND THE BENEFIT OF THE KARNATAKA
CIVIL SERVIES (SERVICE AND KANNADA LANGUAGE
EXAMINATION) RULES, 1974 R/W THE CIRCLAR DATED
03.11.2000 AND 12.09.2001 AT ANNEX-B AND C AND AS PER
THE PETITIONERS THAT IS GRANT OF ONE ADDITIONAL
INCREMENT WITH ALL CONSEQUENTIAL BENEFITS TO THE
PETITIONERS.
THESE PETITIONS COMING ON FOR PRONOUNCEMENT OF
ORDER THIS DAY AFTER HAVING HEARD AND RESERVED FOR
ORDER ON 22.10.2018, THE COURT MADE THE FOLLOWING:
O R D E R
 These writ petitions are filed by the petitioners, some are
retired and some are still in service, seeking direction to the
respondents to extend benefit of one additional increment in
terms of Rule 3 of Karnataka Civil Service (Service & Kannada
Language Examination) Rules 1974, hereinafter referred to as

 492
`the Rules’ for short and also based on orders passed in W P
No.19846-20413/2012 disposed of on 10.8.2012 which is
affirmed by the Division Bench of this Court in W A Nos.2779 &
4858-5434/2013 (S-RES) disposed of on 28.8.2013 and order of
the Hon’ble Apex Court in Special Leave to Appeal (Civil)
Nos.38205-38772/2013 disposed of on 06.01.2014 and in view
of Government Order dated 21.8.2014, which are annexed to the
writ petitions.

2. The facts of the case to be stated in brief are that
petitioners are teaching and non teaching staff of various private
aided educational institutions. Their appointments have been
duly approved with aid by the government. Right from the date
of their initial appointment they have discharged the duties. All
the petitioners have joined the service within the period during
which one additional increment was admissible to these
petitioners in terms of the rules i.e., before 3.9.1987, on which
date Rule 6 of the Rules was amended disentitling the
employees, who joined the service thereafter. It is further case
of the petitioners that in spite of their approaching the

 493
respondents, the respondents have failed to consider the claim
of the petitioners and failed to pass necessary orders thereon. It
is stated that the said issue is already concluded by the orders of
this Court passed by the learned Single Judge in the writ
petition, confirmed by the Division Bench and further affirmed
by the Hon’ble Apex Court.

3. I have heard the learned counsel for the petitioners and
learned counsel for the respondents and perused the writ
papers.

4. The learned counsel for the petitioners submits that
there is total inaction on the part of the respondents in
considering the claim of the petitioners in terms of the Rules and
the orders passed by this Court and the Hon’ble Supreme Court.
Therefore, it is a case where direction is to be issued to the
respondents to consider the claim of the petitioners.

5. On the other hand, the learned counsel for the
respondents submits that since no departmental examinations or
Kannada language examinations were prescribed for employees

 494
in educational institutions receiving maintenance granted from
the State Government, it was thought it fit by the State
Government to pass the amendment with regard to the Act. It is
further submitted that extension of any benefit to the Employees
of Aided Educational Institutions is subject to the economic
capacity of the State placing reliance on Section 49(1) of the
Karnataka Education Act and Rule 3(1)(b) of Karnataka
Education Institution (Recruitment & terms and conditions of
service of employees in private aided Primary & Secondary
Educational Institutions) Rules, 1999. Therefore, it is submitted
that the writ petitions are devoid of merit, liable to be dismissed.

6. This Court in W A Nos.2779, 4868-5434/2013 disposed
of on 28.8.2013 observed in Para-9 of the judgment as follows:
“9. In the instant case, if the State had intended to
deny the benefit of one incentive to the teachers
and non teaching staff of private institutions on
passing of the Kannada Language examination, the
same could have been inserted in Annexure-II of
the Rules. The absence of such an insertion in
Annexure-II coupled with Section 87 read with
clause (b) of Sub-rule(1) of Rule 3 makes clear that

 495
there is no intention on the part of the State
Government to deny such a benefit to the teachers
of the aided institutions, moreover, conditions of
service of staff of private aided institutions could be
varied by the State Government under sub rule (2)
of Rule 3. Such a variation has not been pointed
out in so far as the respondents are concerned. In
that view of the matter, the learned Single Judge
was right in quashing Annexure-A impugned in the
writ petition and directing the State to grant the
benefit of one increment to those teaching and non
teaching staff of the aided institutions, particularly
the respondents herein, on passing of the Kannada
language examination, on par with the Government
schools. In that view of the matter, the impugned
order does not call for any interference in these
appeals. The appeals being devoid of merit are
dismissed”.
The State Government preferred S L P Nos.38205-
38772/2013 before the Hon’ble Apex Court challenging the
above decision of the Division Bench. The Hon’ble Apex court
dismissed the S L Ps. with the following order.

 496
“We are not inclined to interfere with the order
passed by the High Court as the incentive is to be
received by persons who have already retired or
are about to retire for the period from 1974 to
1987.
The special leave petitions are dismissed”.
Therefore, it is clear from the above that the employees
irrespective of government schools or aided institutions who are
appointed or who were in service in between 10.1.1974 &
2.9.1987 and irrespective of their obligation either to pass the
Kannada language examination or not, are entitled to one
additional increment if they are passed Kannada language
examination or said to have passed the same. Hence the
petitioners are entitled to succeed in these writ petitions.

7. Accordingly, all these writ petitions are disposed of. The
respondents are directed to consider the claim of the petitioners
for grant of one additional increment in terms of the Rules and
the orders passed by this Court and which orders are confirmed

 497
by the Apex Court, referred to above. The time for compliance is
within six months from the date of receipt of copy of this order.
Sd/-
JUDGE
AKD
